            // Inline and synchronous on purpose. Anything deferred paints the default theme first.
            (function () {
                var t = localStorage.getItem('demo-theme');
                if (t && t !== 'shadcn') document.documentElement.dataset.theme = t;
                if (localStorage.getItem('demo-dark') === '1') document.documentElement.classList.add('dark');
            })();
        </script>

    <main class="doc-main min-w-0 flex-1 py-8">

<h1 class="doc-h1">Blocks</h1>
<p class="doc-lede">
    Whole sections, composed from the library and meant to be copied and edited. Each carries the
    Unpoly wiring that makes it behave — the part that is easy to leave out and hard to notice
    missing.
</p>
<p class="doc-note"><code>skills/shadcn-unpoly/BLOCKS.md</code> is the same list written for a coding agent: what
    each block is for, what it composes, and the trap in it.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Sign in</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Two attributes here are load-bearing and routinely forgotten:
    <code>up-target="body"</code>, because signing in crosses the auth boundary and the whole
    shell has to be replaced; and <code>up-fail-target</code> on the <b>form</b> rather than a div
    inside it, because <code>[up-disable]</code> disables the antiforgery field too.
</p>
<div data-slot="tabs" data-value="preview" data-orientation="horizontal" class="group/tabs flex gap-2 data-[orientation=horizontal]:flex-col mt-4"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4"><div data-slot="tabs-list" role="tablist" data-variant="default" class="group/tabs-list inline-flex w-fit items-center justify-center rounded-lg p-[3px] text-muted-foreground <h2 class="doc-h2">Data table with a toolbar</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    The filters are a GET form and the URL is the state, so the result is bookmarkable and Back
    works. No client-side filter state exists to get out of sync.
</p>

<p class="doc-note">
    One form per section. A single form across the whole screen makes "Save" mean "save
    everything, including what I did not look at".

<h2 class="doc-h2">Sign up</h2>
<p class="doc-note">The strength meter is rendered by the server from <code>[up-validate]</code>, not computed in the browser: the rule that decides it lives on the server anyway, and a second copy in JavaScript is a second copy to get wrong.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Reset password</h2>
<p class="doc-note">The response is the same whether or not the address exists. Saying "no such account" turns the form into a way of asking whether someone has one.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Verify a code</h2>
<p class="doc-note">Six real inputs; the server sees one field. <code>autocomplete="one-time-code"</code> on the first box is what lets a phone offer the code it has just received.</p>
<div data-slot="tabs" data-value="preview" data-orientation="horizontal" class="group/tabs flex gap-2 data-[orientation=horizontal]:flex-col mt-4"><div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4"><div data-slot="tabs-list" role="tablist" data-variant="default" class="group/tabs-list inline-flex w-fit items-center justify-center rounded-lg p-[3px] text-muted-foreground group-data-[orientation=horizontal]/tabs:h-9 group-data-[orientation=vertical]/tabs:h-fit group-data-[orientation=vertical]/tabs:flex-col data-[variant=line]:rounded-none bg-muted"><button <h2 class="doc-h2">Filters</h2>
<p class="doc-note">The URL is the state, so a filtered view can be sent to a colleague and reached by the back button. Applied filters are chips, because a filter you cannot see is a filter you will blame the data for.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Form wizard</h2>
<p class="doc-note">Each step is a real URL and a real POST, not a client-side index — which is what makes the back button work, a refresh safe, and a half-finished application resumable from a link.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Master and detail</h2>
<p class="doc-note">The shape behind mail, chat and any inbox. Only the detail pane is swapped, so the list keeps its scroll and the address bar always names what is on screen.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Checkout</h2>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Calendar</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Also a table, for the same reason. Month navigation is two links to real URLs, so a month is
    bookmarkable and Back moves through months. For picking a single date use
    <code>DatePicker</code> instead — this one is for showing what is on.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Chat</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    The composer targets <code>.chat-thread:after</code> — append, not replace — so sending adds
    one bubble instead of re-rendering the conversation and losing your scroll. The thread polls
    with an <code>[up-etag]</code>, so a poll that finds nothing costs an empty 304.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">File manager</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Selection is real checkboxes sharing one name, and the bulk bar appears through
    <code>:has(:checked)</code> — CSS reading the boxes rather than a second copy of the selection
    that can drift from them.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Board</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    No drag-and-drop, deliberately. Dragging has no keyboard equivalent unless you build one, and
    the accessible fallback everyone eventually bolts on is exactly this: a control on the card
    saying which column it belongs in. So the fallback is the interface.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Task tracker</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Ticking a box is a POST targeting <code>:origin</code>, so a list of two hundred tasks costs
    one row per tick and nothing below you moves.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Product grid</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Add-to-cart swaps the cart badge, not the grid — which means the badge lives in the chrome and
    <code>&lt;UpChrome Provides=".cart-badge"&gt;</code> is required. Leaving it out is the most
    common bug in this whole pattern: no error, and the swap finds nothing.
</p>

        <form method="get" up-autosubmit up-target="[data-grid]" class="flex gap-2" action="/blocks"><select data-slot="select" data-size="default" data-wrapper-class="w-[170px]" class="w-[170px]" name="sort" aria-label="Sort by"><option data-slot="select-item" value="featured" selected>Featured</option>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Social feed</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Each post is an <code>&lt;article&gt;</code> with a real permalink. Optimistic UI is left out
    on purpose: the round trip is the truth here, and a counter that goes up and then back down is
    worse than one that waits.
</p>

            <p class="mt-3 text-sm leading-relaxed">Shipped the new checkout. Two fewer steps and the error messages finally say what to do.</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Media player</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Built on <code>&lt;audio controls&gt;</code>, which already has seeking, volume, media keys and
    a lock-screen control on a phone. Hand-rolled scrubbers are where audio players lose their
    accessibility.
</p>

<h2 class="doc-h2">Mobile shell</h2>
<p class="doc-note">
    Three things make this feel like an app rather than a page, and all three are easy to leave
    out: <code>100dvh</code> rather than <code>100vh</code>, because a phone's browser chrome
    slides away and <code>vh</code> is frozen at its largest;
    <code>env(safe-area-inset-bottom)</code>, or the last tab sits under the home indicator and
    cannot be tapped; and a tab bar of real links with <code>[up-nav]</code>, so a tab is a URL
    that can be shared and the back button works.
</p>
